# Task Ledger — VOLT Team Portal Rewrite

Source plan: `docs/swarm/VOLT_Portal_PRD.md` v1.6 (authoritative, requirement IDs outrank this ledger), `docs/swarm/constitution.md`, PRD Section 13 epics E1–E11.

## Legend / ledger-wide rules

- **Forbidden files (every task, no exceptions):** `docs/swarm/**`, `.claude/**`, and any file outside the task's own **Allowed files**. Workers are never given write access to these paths. This is stated once here instead of on all 70 rows.
- **Worker agent:** always `worker-implementer` (only worker defined) — omitted from per-task detail, shown once here.
- **Status key:** Not Started · Ready · In Progress · In Check · Needs Rework · Passed · Escalated · Blocked · Done.
- **Init state:** tasks with no dependencies start `Not Started`; tasks with dependencies start `Blocked`.
- **Human-gate tasks** (constitution item 16): checker = `human (George)`. No worker/checker loop — boss-architect records George's sign-off directly on the ledger row (date + method). Attempts/escalation columns are N/A ("—") for these rows.
- **Range dependency shorthand:** `T0xx–T0yy` in a Dependencies cell means "all tasks in that ID range must be Passed," used only for E11 tasks that legitimately fan in across most of the app.
- **External prerequisites (NOT ledger tasks):** Supabase project creation, Google OAuth client credentials, Resend sending-domain DNS verification, and the Vercel CNAME record are actions only George can take outside the swarm. They block specific tasks below (flagged inline) and are tracked as risks in `docs/swarm/state-summary.md`, not as task rows — the swarm cannot create them.
- **Constitution item 17 / BEH-06:** any task touching progress bars, milestones, or meeting history carries an explicit no-streak / no-loss-aversion acceptance bullet — flagged per task below rather than as a separate epic.
- **Row numbers are NAMESPACED PER SESSION, not taken sequentially, whenever more than one session is active.** "Take the next free number" is not a reservation — it reads a file that parallel branches edit independently, and on 2026-07-31 two sessions filed four different tasks under two numbers (T196 and T197) within hours of each other. Sequential reservation immediately above the collision (T198–T205) was rejected by the owner as still too close to the live edge: *"reserve starting at T300, that is a range waaaay safer with no chance of conflict."* **Protocol:** each concurrent session files only into its own block, far above the sequential range. Blocks in force:
  - **T300–T309 — session B** (`claude/t179-mark-day-complete`, and T180/T189 after it).
  - **T320–T329 — session B, continued** (`claude/t305-attendance-over-rsvp` / `claude/t307-bulk-complete-preserve`). Opened 2026-08-01 when T300–T309 filled. **T310–T319 was NOT reused** — it is session A's block and its T310/T311 are VOID placeholders, so taking "the next free number" there would have re-created the exact T196/T197 collision this protocol exists to prevent. It nearly did: the orchestrator filed T310 by increment, spotted the duplicate in a post-write column check, and renumbered.
  - **T310–T319 — session A** (`claude/t183-student-home-loader`, T173/T191/T158). **T310 and T311 are its renumbered T196/T197** — see those rows.
  - **T206–T299 — deliberately left empty.** Do not fill the gap; the gap is the safety margin. A third concurrent session takes T320–T329.
  - Delete any reserved row left unclaimed when a block closes.
- **Astryx props:** every UI task's acceptance includes "props verified against `docs/swarm/astryx-api.md` (DES-19); CLI `npm run astryx -- component <Name>` is a cross-check only."

Epic dependency order (PRD Section 13): **E1 → E2 → E3 → (E4, E5, E6 parallel) → (E7, E8, E9 parallel) → E10 → E11.**

So a student today RSVPs, sees the control update, and the response is discarded on reload. **Worse than an absent UI, because the student believes they have responded** — and no test could catch it, since the local-only handler makes the component behave correctly in every assertion that does not reload. **SEQUENCING — the orchestrator's decision, not the owner's: the student half is HARD-BLOCKED on T170.** Mounting a genuinely-persisting control on a page whose viewer is `'student-placeholder-current-viewer'` would issue **real `rsvps` upserts keyed to a student id that does not exist**. The fake handler is currently what makes that harmless. It says nothing about `RsvpControl`, which is the student-facing control and a different flow. Its mutation path is the same real `submitRsvpChange` (`loaders/outreach.ts:953`) that T157's half uses, so **no *write*-path loader work is needed. Whether the read side needs loader work is UNVERIFIED and this row previously asserted otherwise.** The claim "purely placement" was the orchestrator's, written before `foreman-planner` established that the parallel claim for `ParentRsvp` was false — `ParentRsvp` needs `studentProfileId` and `guardianLinks`, neither of which has any data source (see the T157 row). `RsvpControl`'s own required props have **not** been enumerated against available data, so assume nothing: the packet for this task must establish its read-side needs from the component's signature before scoping, exactly as T157's did. Cheap, mechanical, and it closes a silent-drift class rather than one occurrence. Note also that T157's worker output doc reported `tsc` and eslint but never mentioned `format:check` or `vite build` — worth pinning the required-command list in the worker-output template so a gate cannot be omitted from a report by accident. Packet v2.** The gate built the complete prescription in its own worktree — load seam, block-on-failure rule, §4 seeding, the new parameter — and ran all six gates green (`tsc` 0, `vite build` ✓, eslint 361/0 errors, **72 files / 1771 tests exit 0** including its own scratch criteria). Seven findings, all folded. **MAJOR-3, the most important: F1 as written was VACUOUS** — "`onMarkSessionComplete` called zero times" is free when `remaining` is empty, so it passed green *and stayed green under its own mutation*. Would have been the project's eighth vacuous absence assertion. Now requires four clauses: a fixture with ≥1 `scheduled` session, `expect(confirm).toBeDefined()`, an actual click on the disabled button, and `toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)` on the loader (a mock that never intercepts leaves the real loader rejecting, producing an identical error state). **MAJOR-1/MAJOR-2: the block-on-failure rule reddens six existing tests, and v1 predicted the wrong reason for all of them.** It said `OutreachDetail.test.tsx` would stay green on its resolved-`[]` mock and warned only about a *rejecting* mock; in fact 2 tests fail in the **loading** state (`:1217`, `:1255` — they click confirm synchronously, so the promise has not settled and the button is disabled), and 4 of `MarkEventCompleteDialog.test.tsx`'s 15 fail the same way. **This is the exact inverse of T305**, where a graceful fallback kept the un-mocked file green and the gate had to argue a green count proved nothing; here the new contract announces itself. Fix measured: one `await flushMicrotasks()` before each click, plus an injected loader → `95 passed` and `21 passed`, both exit 0. Authorized explicitly in v2 as the new contract, not a regression. **MINOR-1: the `handleConfirm` guard has no possible criterion** — jsdom's `disabled` attribute suppresses the dispatched click, so F1 passes with the guard deleted. v2 requires the guard as defence-in-depth and says plainly that its mutation is green, so a worker does not rediscover that and conclude it is dead code (the trap T305's W2 set). **MINOR-2: §4's "effectively a no-op" claim was false in one column** — `makeMarkDayComplete`'s upsert also names `recorded_by`/`updated_at`, so the write re-stamps `attendance.recorded_by` from the original recorder to whoever clicked, on a row this path never touched, for a student the coach never saw. Consistent with the documented convention and with T305's W5, but every prior instance involved a coach actively editing that student's row; v2 requires it disclosed in the module doc. Reassuringly, the gate confirmed §4 **cannot** insert a row for a student who previously had none — qualifying requires already having one. **MINOR-3: every citation into the bulk file was stale by ~+23 lines**, v1 having been written pre-T305-merge; all re-verified at `e583e89`. **MINOR-4:** `buildRecordedRowsByStudentId` is deliberately unexported and its file is Forbidden, so the worker must re-derive a four-line filter-and-key locally — in tension with that file's own "REUSE, DON'T RE-DERIVE" doc, now explicitly authorized. **Confirmed correct and not to be re-litigated:** Allowed Files are sufficient (the required parameter produces exactly three errors, all in an allowed file; `buildMarkEventCompletePayload` has zero callers elsewhere — the test T305 v2 failed and this one passes); §3 fits the component without fighting it; blocking the confirm is reachable; the load must be gated on `isOpen` since the dialog is mounted whenever `user !== null`; L1's fixture already exists; P1/P3/P4 discriminate; no import cycle; sonnet tier defensible. **— FAIL on attempt 1 (1 MAJOR), reworked and closed. Full writeup in `verification-log.md`.** **The MAJOR: PostgREST truncation is a third state the design never modelled**, and the one surviving way this dialog could still destroy a row. `config.toml`'s `[api] max_rows = 1000` caps every response and `queryAttendanceForSessions` issues a bare `.select('*').in(...)` with no `.range()`. **A capped response is not an error** — 200 with a partial `Content-Range`, so `result.error` is null, `createLoader` (`loader.ts:174-176`, throws only on `result.error`) resolves the truncated array, and the dialog reaches **`success`** holding only some students' rows. The whole block-on-failure rule never engages because nothing failed, and a truncated-away student is indistinguishable from one who never had a row — so the write nulls their real check-in/hours/method, reproducing byte-for-byte the payload this task exists to eliminate. Verified independently by the orchestrator before acting. **Fixed fail-closed** per the checker's own option (a): a resolve at or above `ATTENDANCE_ROW_CAP` routes to the **error** state, which can only ever block a write and never permit one, turning the design's assumption that the array is complete from assumed into checked. Both mutations reproduced by the orchestrator (`1 failed \| 24 passed` each), plus a companion test one row below the cap proving it is not a blanket block. Loader-side `.range()` pagination filed as **T310**. **Also fixed: the load-effect `isOpen` gate was entirely unpinned** and the packet's cited criterion did not exist — `OutreachDetail.test.tsx:1063` has `user === null`, so this dialog is never mounted there. Removing the gate left both suites green; an ungated load would fire an `attendance` SELECT for every signed-in viewer on every outreach detail page. **Twelve of thirteen adversarial paths were already safe**, on the checker's own fixtures. It also closed two would-be-vacuous mechanisms: `createLoader` throws on `result.error` so a failed query cannot resolve `[]` and masquerade as "nobody attended", and RLS `staff_all on attendance for all` means read visibility equals write reach. **The checker disproved the packet's own F1b claim** — the `handleConfirm` guard IS pinnable; the reason a DOM-level force fails is that Astryx's `Button` guards on the `isDisabled` prop, not the attribute. By mutation layering it proved the rule holds at two independent layers. The worker correctly followed the packet in not inventing a criterion; the packet was wrong, not the worker. **The worker found a fact the packet did not know:** this dialog is mounted for any signed-in user and its content sits in the DOM while closed, so its first loading state leaked `aria-busy` onto every page; its `isOpen` render gate is load-bearing and reddens **two** pre-existing a11y tests (T157 parent, T169 student), not the one it reported. **`aria-busy` cleared against item 2** — a native ARIA attribute already used at `AttendancePanel.tsx:835`, `Leaderboard.tsx:483` and `OutreachDetail.tsx:1801`. **P2 was not unpinned** — the checker ran the mutation in its own worktree, which item 23 authorizes. **No fresh checker round on the rework** (item 25): remedy specified by the checker, ~4 lines plus three tests in Allowed Files, both mutations reproduced directly. Same posture as T302/T303. The work shipped in **PR #28** (`bc53aab`, *"W1 (check in) — T321, T161, T320, T403 steps 1-3"*) and no one updated the three rows afterwards. **Original row text follows.** **Fixed by `.range()` pagination, the stronger of the two options the row offered.** `makeLoadAttendanceForSessions` now pages until a SHORT page returns; a full page means "at least this many", never "exactly this many", so an exact-multiple result costs one extra empty request rather than dropping what followed. **`.order('id')` is load-bearing, not cosmetic** — page N+1 is an offset into a result set and Postgres guarantees no ordering without an explicit `order by`, so paging an unordered query can return one row twice and never return another; `id` is the uuid PK. A page-count bound turns a server that ignores `.range()` into a diagnosable throw rather than an unbounded loop, and it throws rather than returning what it gathered — returning a partial set would reintroduce the very truncation being removed. **Scope discovery: this loader is shared across three workflows** — `endMeeting.ts:191` (W3) imports it and three W2 pages consume it — so six tests in `endMeeting.test.ts` and `AttendancePanel.test.tsx` stubbed the old chain and broke on `.order()`. **Owner authorized crossing that boundary** rather than weakening the fix; both edits are stub-only, no production change in either file. **Two follow-ups filed: T401** (the `ATTENDANCE_ROW_CAP` guard this row was meant to delete is now a false positive) and **T402** (`loaders/outreach.ts:745` carries the identical defect, which T320 never named). | T201 | Data consistency (`is_active` family) — **CLOSED 2026-08-05; owner ruled, fixed, mutation-proved** | a deactivated student's real historical hours sit in `v_student_hours` (no `is_active` filter) but are invisible everywhere a consumer reads through `v_student_goal_projection` instead (`where s.is_active`) | orchestrator | self-verified (4 mutations + live PG16.13) | T191 (owner ruled 2026-07-31 — "no bar," this row unaffected either way), T184 (landed), T189 (filed, same family) | 1 | **Filed while packeting T191 (item 20)** | **Found while packeting T191** (`src/lib/supabase/loaders/parentHome.ts:482-483`'s `confirmedHours: scope?.confirmedHours ?? 0` treats a deactivated linked student as having zero hours). `v_student_hours` (`supabase/migrations/20260717000003_metric_views.sql:3-19`) is a real, already-shipped view with **no** `is_active` filter and may hold nonzero real hours for her; `v_student_goal_projection` (`supabase/migrations/20260723000001_dashboard_views.sql:334`, `where s.is_active`) is what actually hides them from every current consumer. **Unlike T184's fix, relaxing `v_student_goal_projection`'s own filter is unsafe** — `resolveStudentScope`'s `null` return for an inactive student is exactly the signal T184's `{kind:'inactive'}` branch on `StudentHome` depends on, so any fix here needs a new, separate read, not a change to that view. **Scope genuinely unknown, same posture as T189**: establish where, if anywhere (a parent's card, a coach-facing view, nowhere), a deactivated student's historical hours should be shown before designing a fix — not decided by this row. Sequence after T191 resolves in the ledger, since T191's own fix touches the same card. **DIAGNOSIS 2026-08-05 — this row asked for scoping before a fix, and that is what this is. No code changed.** **Premise re-verified against the LIVE view definitions, not the originals the row cites** (`v_student_hours` was create-or-replaced by T322's `20260804000000`): it still has no `is_active` filter — it never joins `students` at all, so one is not even possible — and `v_student_goal_projection` still ends `where s.is_active`. Both halves hold. **Surveyed every consumer. Three of four are SAFE, and the row did not know which:** `leaderboard.ts:138` reads `v_student_hours` unfiltered but joins `v_leaderboard_students`, which is `where is_active`; `reports.ts:414` carries three `.eq('is_active', true)` roster filters; `coachHome.ts:350` filters in JS (`sumConfirmedHours` on `student.isActive`). **The leak is `v_team_hours`** (`20260722000000_membership_views.sql`), which sums `v_student_hours` joined only to `student_teams` — **no `is_active` filter at any level** — and feeds `dashboard.ts:308` → CoachHome's **"Hours by team"** widget. **MEASURED on live PG16.13**, two students on one team who both attended the same 4 h outreach session, one since deactivated: **"Hours by team" reports 8.0 h while the students a coach can actually see sum to 4.0 h.** The coach sees a team total DOUBLE the roster shown, with nothing on screen to account for the gap. That is the `is_active` family's third instance, made concrete. **NOT FIXED, deliberately — the remaining question is a product ruling this row explicitly reserves:** should a departed student's historical hours still count toward their team's total? **(a) No** — filter them, so the total always equals the visible roster; consistent, but understates what the team really did. **(b) Yes** — the hours were genuinely volunteered for that team; keep them, but the total legitimately will not match the roster sum and needs a visible explanation or coaches read it as a bug. **(c) Yes, and surface departed students' hours per-student** so the arithmetic reconciles — largest change, and note this row's own warning that `v_student_goal_projection`'s filter CANNOT simply be relaxed, since T184's `{kind:'inactive'}` branch depends on its null-for-inactive signal; it needs a new read. **OWNER RULED 2026-08-05: the hours COUNT** — verbatim *"that's actual work that was done regardless if that person is no longer with the team or not. That's like asking if your work done today counts for my token usage if i deactivate your session."* Owner chose **(c)** — keep the total AND surface departed members per-student — and delegated the goal-column sub-decision. **So `v_team_hours`'s 8.0 was RIGHT and HoursTab was wrong**: it hid the person, and the hours went with them. **NO MIGRATION** — `v_team_hours`, `v_student_hours` and `v_student_goal_projection` are all untouched, so T184's null-for-inactive signal (this row's own stated hazard) is unaffected. **The fix was cheap because the data was already there:** `queryHoursStudentHours` reads `v_student_hours` UNFILTERED, so departed students' hours were already being fetched and then discarded by the roster query's `.eq('is_active', true)`. Removing that filter (and selecting `is_active` so rows can be marked) was the whole data change. `buildStudentRows` keeps an inactive student ONLY when they have hours, so a departed student with nothing never appears. **Goal column, delegated and decided:** a past member shows `—` for goal and %, contributes 0 to the goal subtotal, and renders no ProgressBar. Counting their goal would make a team's "% to goal" DROP because somebody left — trading one wrong number for another. Confirmed and planned hours still count in full, which is what makes the subtotal reconcile. **4 mutations replayed.** Dropping past members again → RED; giving one a real goal → RED. **Removing the goal-subtotal filter alone → GREEN, and that is correct, not a gap:** `goalHours: 0` already enforces the rule, so the filter is a second, independent mechanism — applying BOTH mutations together is the only way to defeat it, and the subtotal assertion catches that. Defence in depth, each single-point failure pinned. **One of my own T163 tests was INVERTED**, not deleted: `makeLoadHoursData` *"scopes students to active only"* asserted the very filter that was the defect. It now asserts the filter is absent, and notes that the participation loader legitimately still filters. Two fake-client doubles were reshaped to the real `.select(...).order(...)` call rather than left permissive. **T071 — Shared Supabase client + auth/session surface + typed loader seam (`src/lib/supabase/**`)**
Objective: Create the single shared, typed Supabase client module the whole frontend will consume, closing the cross-cutting gap independently flagged by T018, T020, T021, T034, T035, and T056 (each confirmed via grep that `src/` contains zero `createClient`/`@supabase/supabase-js` usages, and each shipped an injectable `loadData`-style seam defaulting to honest fixture/null data instead of fabricating a backend). This task provides three things, all new files, nothing else:
1. **Client singleton** — `src/lib/supabase/client.ts` (or equivalent under `src/lib/supabase/`): exactly one `createClient` call site in all of `src/`, lazily initialized, reading `import.meta.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL` / `import.meta.env.VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` — the exact env-var names T015 already committed to `.env.example`. Anon key only; the words "service role" must never appear as a capability here (constitution item 5, BLOCKER).
2. **Auth/session surface** — thin, typed wrappers shaped to slot into `guards.tsx`'s existing `AuthContextValue` contract (`user`, `isLoading`, `login`, `loginWithGoogle`, `logout` — read `src/app/guards.tsx` lines 51–70 before designing) WITHOUT modifying `guards.tsx` in this task: initial `getSession()` retrieval, an `onAuthStateChange` subscription helper (returning an unsubscribe), `signInWithPassword`, `signInWithOAuth({ provider: 'google', options: { redirectTo } })` (the exact call `guards.tsx`'s own TODO comment anticipates), `signOut`, and a role-resolution helper that reads the signed-in user's `profiles.role` (the T019 trigger guarantees a `profiles` row exists for any legitimately-invited user; a missing row is the AUTH-04 no-access case and must surface as a distinct typed result, not an exception swallow). Export the REAL AUTH-05 role vocabulary as a type (`'admin' | 'coach' | 'student' | 'parent'`, matching `role_enum` in T009's migration verbatim) and document — do not resolve — the known mismatch with `guards.tsx`'s stale `Role` union (`'admin' | 'staff' | 'volunteer' | 'coach'`); reconciliation belongs to the follow-up wiring task that is allowed to edit `guards.tsx`.
3. **Typed loader seam** — the convergent pattern all six pages independently arrived at is `(args) => Promise<Data | null>` injected as an optional prop with an obviously-fake default (see `LoadInviteFn` in `src/pages/accept-invite/types.ts`, `LoadNoAccessDataFn` in `src/pages/no-access/types.ts`, `KioskDisplayTokenLoader`/`KioskTallyLoader` in `src/pages/meetings/Kiosk.tsx`, `LoadParticipationDataFn` in `src/pages/reports/ParticipationTab.tsx`). Provide the generic building block those future real loaders will be written with: a small helper (e.g. `createLoader`) that wraps a query against the shared client with normalized error semantics — transport/query errors reject with a typed error carrying DES-16-compatible information (what happened + what to do, no apologies), "no rows" resolves `null` where the seam's return type allows it, and NO catch-and-return-fake-data path exists anywhere. Plus a `types.ts` with hand-authored row interfaces ONLY for the tables/views the six flagged pages need (`invites`, `students`, `teams`, `profiles`, `event_sessions`, `attendance`, `v_student_participation` at minimum), with column names/types transcribed from the real migration files under `supabase/migrations/` — types only; re-deriving any metric formula or RLS logic in TypeScript is a BLOCKER (constitution items 3, DATA-01). This task does NOT write any page-specific loader implementation and does NOT touch any page file — per-page wiring is the follow-up series.
Why E3: PRD Section 13 defines only E1–E11 and none is a generic infra epic; inventing an E12 would fork the PRD-derived epic vocabulary for one task. E3 (Auth + invites) is the best-fit home — this task operationalizes T015's auth provider config into runnable client code, and the gap was first flagged at T016/T018's close-outs in this same epic. T016a already set the precedent of E3 housing corrective/wiring infra.
Allowed files: `src/lib/supabase/**` (all new — implementation, `types.ts`, barrel `index.ts`, and unit tests colocated as `src/lib/supabase/*.test.ts`), plus `package.json`/`package-lock.json` strictly for adding `@supabase/supabase-js` (allowlisted, constitution item 9 — report the exact version taken; if its peer ranges force any other bump, report it explicitly, never take it silently, per the T002a rule).
Forbidden (explicitly, beyond the ledger-wide rule): `src/app/guards.tsx`, `src/app/router.tsx`, `src/app/AppShell.tsx`, all `src/pages/**`, `src/theme/**`, `supabase/**`, `.env.example`, `.github/**`, `vite.config.ts`.
Dependencies: T015 (env-var names + auth provider config this client must match — Passed), T019 (guarantees the `profiles`-row-at-first-sign-in invariant the role-resolution helper depends on; also the live confirmation of the real role vocabulary — Passed). → Ready.
External-prerequisite posture (George's real Supabase project does not exist yet — same posture as T015/T061, staged-not-blocked): the module must be fully functional-once-credentialed and honest-when-not. Concretely: an `isSupabaseConfigured()` predicate; when either env var is blank/absent, `getSupabaseClient()` throws a typed `SupabaseNotConfiguredError` (fail loud) and the app must still BOOT (lazy init — module import alone never throws, or the whole app dies at startup for every dev without a `.env`). Never fabricate a placeholder URL/key that could be mistaken for real, and never return fixture data from this module — honest fixtures live in the pages' defaults, not in the data layer.
Acceptance:
- Exactly one `createClient` call site under `src/` (grep-verifiable); env access uses the two T015 names only; TypeScript strict, no `any` in exported signatures.
- Unconfigured mode verified by unit test: blank env → `isSupabaseConfigured()` false, `getSupabaseClient()` throws the typed error, module import does not throw.
- Auth surface verified by unit tests against a stubbed transport (no real network in tests): session retrieval, auth-state subscription fires + unsubscribes cleanly, `signInWithOAuth` invoked with provider `'google'` and a caller-supplied `redirectTo`, sign-out clears state, role-resolution helper returns the typed role for a present `profiles` row and the distinct no-profile result (AUTH-04 path) for a missing one.
- Loader helper verified by unit tests: transport error → typed rejection with DES-16-compatible fields; no-rows → `null`; success → typed data; provably no fake-data fallback branch (checker should read the source, not just run tests).
- Row types in `types.ts` cross-checked column-by-column against the real migration SQL files (checker re-derives this independently; a column name/type absent from the migrations is a MAJOR); zero metric-formula arithmetic anywhere in the module (constitution item 3 — BLOCKER).
- Secret hygiene: no literal URL/key/JWT-shaped string anywhere in the new files or in `dist/` (checker greps the built bundle; the three-pattern T015 methodology); `.env` still absent from the repo; `.env.example` byte-unchanged.
- `npm run build`/`typecheck`/`lint`/`format:check`/`test` all exit 0; CI bundle-size gate re-verified green (supabase-js lands in the eager entry chunk — if it blows the NFR-04 budget, flag for a code-splitting decision, do not silently raise the budget).
- D001-method forbidden-file check: only `src/lib/supabase/**` + the two package files changed.
Evidence: file list; grep output for the single-`createClient` and secret-hygiene checks (src + dist); full test-runner output; build/typecheck/lint/format:check output; bundle-size gate output with before/after gzip numbers; `package.json`/`package-lock.json` diff summary with exact `@supabase/supabase-js` version; explicit column-by-column citation mapping each exported row type to its migration file/line.
Out of scope, declared now so nobody improvises it: swapping `guards.tsx`'s placeholder `AuthProvider` internals for this module (follow-up corrective task, T016a pattern — it also owns the `Role`-union reconciliation and the D003-class risk of breaking `theme.smoke.test.tsx`-adjacent tests when real session checks mount); wiring each of the six pages' `loadData` seams to real loaders (one small follow-up per page or per epic, each independently checkable); generated `Database` types via `supabase gen types` (CLI unavailable in this sandbox — hand-authored minimal types now, regeneration can supersede them later without breaking the seam).

T010's migration defines `notes text not null` with no default, following a literal reading of PRD 8.1's "no null-marker = NOT NULL" rule — but MTG-02's own field spec doesn't require notes to enable the "Create meetings" button, implying it's optional. This task's `event_sessions` INSERT must either always supply a value (even an empty string) or this task must ship a small additive migration making the column nullable/defaulted — do not edit T010's migration file per constitution item 10. Flag the choice explicitly in this task's worker output.

**T032 — `checkin` Edge Function**
Objective: HMAC-SHA256(sessionId + 60s bucket, `CHECKIN_HMAC_SECRET`) token + derived 6-char short code (MTG-06); validates current/previous bucket (≤2 min), session liveness, team scope; upserts `attendance` (`method='qr'`); auto present/late per 10-min grace (OQ-3); idempotent duplicate check-in (MTG-09); short-code rate limit 5/min/user.
Allowed files: `supabase/functions/checkin/**`.

| T325 | Bug (responsive) — **audit LIVE-006** | The mobile student RSVP row collapses its content at 390×844 | **MERGED** — see `verification-log.md` | — | — | 1 | **VERIFIED 2026-08-03 by direct measurement — this row previously read "Plausible, not independently verified" and that is now superseded.** Measured in **real Chromium at 390×844** via a throwaway Playwright rig (the T131/T142 convention: real dev server, real provider stack, rig deleted afterwards and the deletion declared). **The defect is REAL: 213px of horizontal overflow** — `document.scrollWidth` **603** against `clientWidth` **390**. **The audit's description is misleading, and the mechanism is not what it implies.** Nothing "collapses": the row **overflows**, and the culprit is **not** the RSVP `SegmentedControl` (the obvious suspect — the orchestrator guessed it from source and was wrong). It is the student row's **`endContent` action span**, `display: flex` with **`flex-shrink: 0`**, measured **563px wide inside a 342px `ListItem` whose own `flex-shrink` is 1**. It therefore refuses to compress or wrap. It is wide because the button labels embed the full event title — *"Hide session details – Canned Food Drive"* (563px) and *"Mark attendance – Canned Food Drive"* (241px) — the accessible-name pattern T131/T132 deliberately established, so **the labels are correct and must not simply be truncated**; the shrink/wrap behaviour is what is wrong. **Structural root cause:** the coach table got a full `isNarrow` branch under T130/T132; the **student view has none** — `isNarrow` appears nowhere in it, so it renders one layout at every width. **Blocked while T190 is in flight** — same file (`OutreachList.tsx`). **DIAGNOSED FURTHER 2026-08-04 — and the obvious fix does NOT work, measured.** The `endContent` `HStack` **already has `wrap="wrap"`**, so "make it wrap" is not the fix. Adding `maxWidth="100%"` — the component-level prop, item 11's first escalation step — was applied and measured: **still 213px overflow, unchanged.** The DOM shows why: our props land correctly, but the constraining element is **Astryx's own `endContent` slot wrapper**, one level above ours — a `span` at `flex-shrink: 0`, `max-width: none`, sized to its content at 563px. Our `HStack` inherits that 563px box, so its `maxWidth: 100%` resolves against 563 and its `wrap` never fires. Measured chain: `span(563, shrink 0, maxW none)` > `div.astryx-stack(563, wrap, maxW 100%)` > `button(285)` + `button(265)`, inside a **342px** `li`. **Each button would fit at 342px if wrapping engaged** — the content is not too wide, the box is unconstrained. **Shortening the labels is NOT available:** `Button.label` is the visible text AND the accessible name (`astryx-api.md:1811`), so trimming it would undo T131/T132's distinguishable-accessible-name work; and nothing on `ListItem` exposes the slot's width. **This therefore needs an item-11 escalation decision, not a prop change.** Options: **(a)** move the two actions out of `endContent` into the row body / expanded content — stays within component composition and is the **orchestrator's recommendation**; **(b)** `xstyle`; **(c)** custom CSS targeting Astryx's slot. **(a) is the only one that does not reach into a component's internals.** **Not packeted** — the escalation step is a judgement worth stating before building. **CLOSED 2026-08-04. Owner chose option A** (`auto-mode-decisions.md`, 2026-08-04): move the actions into the row body rather than override the slot or write custom CSS. **Measured after: 213px → 0**, all five buttons present, labels unchanged. **Shipped with NO new test, deliberately** — jsdom does no layout, two structural tests were measured **vacuous**, and a third was redundant with existing T170/T126 guards. The evidence is the measurement. | Plausible, not independently verified. Reported from a live viewport measurement. | No |

| T400 | Missing capability (found while scoping T321) — **W1 block** | A student who cannot scan at all has no session id, so the kiosk's short code alone cannot check them in | orchestrator (STANDARD tier) | orchestrator (12 mutations replayed, all red at exit 1) | T196 (merged) | 0 | **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-04 — option (a), the live-session picker.** ~~Owner-ruled 2026-08-02 — option (a). Sequenced into T196's wave.~~ | **This is the half of audit LIVE-002 that T321 could not close, and neither the audit nor the T321 row saw it.** Both describe the fix as *"UI-only on a working backend"*. That is true for the expiry case — the URL already carries `?s=`, so T321's form reuses it. It is **false** for the case the audit actually named (*"a student who cannot scan has no fallback"*): `validateCheckinRequest` (`supabase/functions/checkin/validation.ts`) rejects any body without a uuid `session_id`, and `verifyShortCode` (`supabase/functions/checkin/hmac.ts:133-145`) HMACs the presented code over `` `${sessionId}:${bucket}` `` — a short code is meaningful **only** relative to one specific session. **The kiosk (`Kiosk.tsx`) displays the QR and the 6-char code but never the session id in readable form**, so a student reading the TV has no way to supply one. T321 deliberately does not render a form when `s` is absent, because that form could only ever fail. **Three candidate fixes, none UI-only:** (a) a live-session picker on `/checkin` — needs a loader listing open sessions, adjacent to T196's `LiveConsole` work; (b) the kiosk also displays a short session identifier the student types; (c) the edge function resolves a code against currently-live sessions — a backend change in `supabase/functions/**`, which **W1 does not own**. **(c) needs an owner call before anyone scopes it: it weakens the code's session binding and widens the 5/min rate-limit surface.** **RULING (`auto-mode-decisions.md`, "George's ruling on T400"): option (a), the live-session picker.** `/checkin` offers a list of currently-open sessions; the student taps the event they are at, which supplies the session id, then types the kiosk code. **(b) is closed** (two things to type on a phone in a noisy shop). **(c) is closed** — it would break the code's session binding, letting a code issued for one event match another running concurrently, while widening the 5/min rate-limit surface; it also lives in `supabase/functions/**`, which W1 does not own. **Sequencing: (a) needs a currently-open-sessions loader, which T196 must build anyway — so this is folded into T196's wave and must not be started before it.** **⚠️ THAT SEQUENCING CLAIM WAS FALSE — measured 2026-08-04 before starting.** T196 shipped and built **no such loader**. Every loader it produced takes a `sessionId` and returns one session: `makeLoadEndMeetingSummary` (`endMeeting.ts:288`), `makeOnEndMeeting` (`:373`), `makeOnEditAttendance` (`:448`), and `makeLoadLiveConsoleData` (`kiosk.ts:430`). Nothing anywhere in `loaders/` listed open sessions — grep for `openSessions`/`liveSessions`/`'live'` returned **zero** hits. So the row understated its own scope: T400 was never 'UI on a loader T196 leaves behind', it had to build that loader. The dependency on T196 was still real (do not mount a picker onto a fixture console), just not for the stated reason. **DELIVERED:** new `makeLoadOpenCheckinSessions` + `OPEN_SESSION_GRACE_MS` in **W1's** `loaders/checkin.ts`, and a picker in `CheckinResult.tsx` rendered exactly where T321 withholds its form. **Two design calls worth recording.** (1) **Window: ±2h on `ends_at`/`starts_at`, symmetric.** The owner's stated case is a student *leaving* who forgot to check in, and students also arrive early, so a start-time-only window would miss the very case that motivated the row. (2) **Filtered on `timestamptz`, never `session_date`.** A `date` comparison must pick a timezone to mean 'today', and a 6-9pm US-Central build night is already tomorrow in UTC — the shop's own meeting would list as tomorrow's or not at all. **Team scoping is left to RLS** (`event_sessions`'s `own_or_linked_read`, `20260717000002_rls.sql:180-189`), not duplicated client-side where it would silently diverge. **Verified: 12 mutations, all red at exit 1** — including a positional-join mutant caught by a deliberately reversed events fixture (the wrong-meeting-name defect class) and a 'picker always sends the first session' mutant caught by asserting against the *second* entry. Suite **2010 → 2024** (+14), 80 files, exit 0; tsc 0, eslint 0 errors. | No |

| T512 | Debt (process) — **filed 2026-08-05 on the owner's suggestion during PR #89 review** | `WORKFLOWS.md`'s open-row counts are hand-recounted from the document itself; derive them from the ledger with a script instead | *not yet packeted* | — | none | 0 | **Filed 2026-08-05** | **Filed because hand-recounting produced two wrong numbers in one PR.** `docs/swarm/WORKFLOWS.md`'s assignment table carries an open-row count per workflow. PR #89 recomputed those counts by reading `WORKFLOWS.md` — which is the artifact under repair — and shipped **two** defects the owner caught in review. (1) The helper treated `NARROWED` as a closed status, so T333 was silently dropped from every count and W9 read `3 — all human gates` while the same cell described T333's machine-workable ETL half. (2) The helper split the file into sections on `^## (W\d+) — `; W10 is the **last** workflow section, so its section ran to EOF and swallowed the Coordination-rules block-allocation table that follows, counting `T333`, `T600-699` and `T700-799` as W10 rows — W10 went 8 → 9 when the true figure was **6** (8 listed, T157 and T301 merged). Both defects are the same class: a count derived from prose, where a parsing miss produces a plausible number instead of an error. **The owner's framing, which is the fix:** *"the ledger is the only place a row's status actually lives, so counts belong derived from it — even a scripted count would beat hand-recounting a doc that four machines are appending to."* **Shape of the work:** a script reading `docs/swarm/task-ledger.md`'s pipe table (ID in column 1, Status in column 8), mapping each row ID to a workflow via the block allocation, and emitting open counts per workflow. Two requirements carried from the failures above — it must treat `NARROWED` as **open**, and it must **fail loudly** rather than return a number when a row ID maps to no workflow block or a status string matches none of the known keywords. A count that cannot be computed must say so; that is the same rule the `mutation-replay` skill enforces. Whether it runs in CI or by hand before a table edit is open. Suggested Allowed Files: `scripts/**`, `docs/swarm/WORKFLOWS.md`. **MEASURED 2026-08-05, and it raises the bar for this row: the table is NOT column-consistent, so "Status is column 8" is not a safe parse.** Splitting all 278 `| Tnnn |` rows on unescaped pipes gives **248 rows with the header's 10 columns, and 30 that disagree** — 2 with 9, 21 with 11, 6 with 12, 1 with 13. Found while updating T604 by column index: the patch asserted 10 columns and aborted rather than writing to the wrong cell. **A positional parser must therefore locate `Status` by header name and FAIL LOUDLY on any row whose column count does not match the header — never index blindly**, which is the same "a miss must error, not return a plausible number" requirement this row already carries. Normalising the 30 rows is the obvious companion fix and may be the cheaper half. | No |
